#+TODO: TODO(t) ONGOING(o) WAIT(w@) PENDING(p@) | DONE(d!) CANCELLED(c@) DEFERRED(f@)
#+COLUMNS: %40ITEM %TODO %EFFORT %CLOCKSUM(Clocked)
# Hiring rule (SEGREGATION LAW, Owner 18:14Z): the assignee (AUTHOR) is clocked in to ONGOING by AUTOMATION at the exact dispatch (Owner 2026-10-26 10:42Z — never by hand) and flips the item to PENDING with :DELIVERED: evidence on delivery; the NEXT seat (REVIEWER) reviews the branch read-only; the CI merger (MERGER) lands it only on the reviewer's APPROVED verdict. Marking DONE is the duty REVIEWER's when everything is clear (Owner 2026-10-26 10:55Z); CANCELLED/DEFERRED stay Lead-only.
#+PROPERTY: Effort_ALL 0 0:25 0:50 1:00 2:00 4:00
#+PROPERTY: ORDERED t
# TIME LAW (Owner 2026-10-26 10:42 #hq): strict time discipline, Org-powered.
# 1. Every TODO assigned by the team lead sets :EFFORT: (from the
#    Effort_ALL ladder; outside the ladder needs the Owner's word).
# 2. The BEGINNING of a task = the EXACT time its prompt is sent to the
#    member. AUTOMATION marks it, never the member: the item flips to
#    ONGOING and the send instant enters its :LOGBOOK: as an open Org
#    clock. Emacs path: the agent-org prompt-send hook
#    (agent-team-clock.el). CI path: the dispatch-step clock-in
#    (clock_in.py — canonical in khaalidrafi/agent-team.el).
# 3. MANUAL EXCEPTION: web/cloud models never pass through agent-org —
#    whoever dispatches pastes the ONGOING flip + :LOGBOOK: clock with
#    the exact send time.
# 4. A TODO closed without clock evidence bounces at review.
# 5. The closing report carries one line: :EFFORT: vs clocked variance.
# REVIEW CLOCK LAW (Owner 2026-10-26 10:55 #hq; Org manual "Breaking
# down tasks" + clocking: child TODOs nest one level under the parent,
# statistics cookies count the subtree, a parent's clocksum aggregates
# its subtree's clocks):
# 1. Every lead-assigned work TODO carries a REVIEW TODO nested
#    underneath it — one Org level deeper, headline
#    `*** TODO review: <parent title>', with its own :EFFORT: (inside
#    its :PROPERTIES: drawer, the reviewer's rung of the Effort_ALL
#    ladder) and its own :LOGBOOK: clock.
# 2. FINISH time and REVIEW time are SEPARATE clocks: work clock on the
#    parent, review clock on the child; the variance line reports them
#    apart (columnview rolls the child's clock into the parent's
#    %CLOCKSUM).
# 3. The REVIEWER sets DONE — on the review TODO first, then on the
#    parent — when everything is clear: verdict + green gate + evidence.
#    The author never grades their own work; CANCELLED/DEFERRED stay
#    Lead-only.
# 4. Automation: work dispatches clock the parent (clock_in.py work
#    mode — never a review child); review dispatches clock the review
#    child (review mode; the marker may match the child's evidence
#    lines, so cite branch=/author= in its body).

  undefined exactly when the document has no audit model. So the delivered
  behaviour was a REQUEST TO A GUESSED URL, tolerated by a 404 catch — against
  the law AGENTS.md:63 states for the same surface ("never a hand-written
  /api/audit/") and weaker than transitionRecord/fetchHistory, which refuse.
  Real risk, not cosmetics: on a deployment where some other app owns that
  segment the client reads the wrong log and renders it as this record's audit
  trail, with no error anywhere.
  Fix, test-first: fetchAudit now throws ApiError naming the missing evidence
  when no discovered path is supplied and fires zero requests (same refusal
  shape as the R-001 pair); ModelPage gates the whole audit half on
  findAuditModel() resolving, so a log-less backend degrades to the lifecycle
  half without a probe or a toast. docs/custom-actions.md's stale
  "fixed /api/audit/ surface" bullet rewritten to state the refusal.
  Proven both directions: app/src/lib/__tests__/actions.test.ts gained 2
  tests (refusal + zero network calls; the 3 existing reads now pass the
  fixture-DISCOVERED listPath, so no test pins a hand-written URL any more)
  and lifecycleMount.test.ts gained a WIRE-LEVEL test — with the audit model
  present the sub-view GETs
  http://api.test/api/audit/?object_type=order&object_id=7 and renders the
  entry; with the model stripped from the same document it makes no /audit/
  request and no toast. The negative is time-boxed (150 ms window — the
  interval in which the old fallback was measured to fire), so it is evidence
  and not a race. Reverted-source check: these tests are RED on the pre-fix
  tree (2 fail in actions.test.ts, 1 in lifecycleMount.test.ts) and GREEN with
  the fix — the mount test's FIRST run was a false pass (waitFor settled
  before the guessed request landed); caught and hardened before committing.
  Gate green at this HEAD: pytest 212/212, manage.py check 0 issues,
  makemigrations --check "No changes detected", vitest 255/255 (19 files).
  State untouched — stays PENDING (DONE is Lead-only).

- Note taken on [2026-10-21 Wed 11:11 UTC] :: THE FLOAT DIVERGENCE FIXED,
  both sides, one commit (this seat, ai/turn-20261021-1101; state untouched —
  PENDING stands, DONE is the Lead's gate). The [10:40] note above recorded
  the finding as unreachable-by-product and documented it. It is reachable by
  CLIENT PROJECTS, which is the product: a FloatField goes out of DRF's
  JSONRenderer as a JSON number, layer 1 parses it and `String()`s it ("1"),
  layer 2 formats the same double with Python `str()` ("1.0") — so a real
  customer export gets two buttons and two files, silently. Fixed on the wrong
  side (the server; the browser's rendering is what the user sees in the table):
  - demo-backend/shop/csv_export.py: new `js_number_text()` — ECMA-262
    Number::toString layout over Python's shortest round-trip repr (integral
    floats, the -6 < n <= 21 switch points, unpadded exponents, String(-0)="0",
    NaN/Infinity named as JS names them). `csv_cell` uses it for `float` only;
    Decimal still writes its own serial form ("decimals never re-flowed" holds).
  - SECOND DIVERGENCE FOUND WHILE FIXING THE FIRST, same file, previously
    unreported: the formula guard was keyed on the RENDERED TEXT, not on the
    value being text — contradicting this file's own docstring ("formula guard
    on text only") and app/src/lib/csv.ts, which literally checks
    `typeof value === "string"`. A negative float in exponent form ("-1e-9") is
    not NUMERIC_SHAPED, so the server apostrophised what the browser left bare.
    Live on HEAD for any FloatField holding |x| < 1e-6 or >= 1e17, i.e. before
    the repr fix it could not even be reached by test. Now `if is_text and
    is_formula_text(text)`.

    golden document gained a `weight` column (1.0, -0.5, 1e-7). RED FIRST,
    measured: 27 failed / 53 passed on HEAD's writer before the fix.

    asserted against `csvCell`/`buildCsv` — this side was green immediately,

- Note taken on [2026-10-21 Wed 12:41 UTC] :: Root cause of the CONTAINER-BOOT
  harness row (the eighth fresh container where the seat had to install
  deps itself before the gate could run) — MEASURED, and it overturns my own
  12:28Z guess. The boot install is NOT silently failing:
  /home/agent/.local/lib/python3.12/site-packages/__editable__.admincraft-0.1.2.pth
  has birth time 12:35:36.071 in THIS container, PID1 start 12:35:33.632
  (`stat -c %w` vs `/proc/1`) — so agent-team.yml:509 ran and pip exited 0.
  What breaks it is that the launcher then pins a different HOME
  (agent-team.yml:519 `env HOME=/usr/local/lib/qoder-home qoder …`), Python's
  user-site follows $HOME (`site.getusersitepackages()` returns
  /usr/local/lib/qoder-home/.local/… with this seat's HOME), so the boot .pth
  is on a path the seat interpreter never reads: first `import admincraft`
  → ModuleNotFoundError, `app/node_modules` → 0 entries. The discarded stderr
  and `|| true` are NOT the cause (pip genuinely succeeds); the cause is the
  HOME split between the install line and the launch line. Discriminating
  tests, all run here: (1) writing a plain one-line `admincraft-src.pth`
  containing /workspace/plugin/src into the pinned user site → pytest 285/285
  + manage.py check "no issues (0 silenced)" — the editable machinery
  (dist-info, import hooks) is NOT needed, a path .pth is sufficient; (2) the
  same under `PYTHONPATH=/workspace/plugin/src` WITHOUT the .pth → 5 failed,
  276 passed, 4 errors, so a bare PYTHONPATH is NOT sufficient.
  ONE-LINER FOR THE LEAD (agent-team.yml is lead-owned; cited, not touched),
  safest first: make line 509 use the SAME HOME as line 519 —
  `env HOME=/usr/local/lib/qoder-home python -m pip install -e /workspace/plugin
  --no-deps -q` — so the .pth lands where the seat interpreter looks. The
  HOME-independent variant (write to the shared prefix) needs root: this seat
  `touch /usr/local/lib/python3.12/site-packages/admincraft-src.pth` →
  Permission denied (whoami=agent), so it means a chown or a root `python -m
  pip` in the image build. `npm ci` in app/ has NO boot line at all — `grep
  -n "npm " .github/workflows/agent-team.yml` hits only 64-65 and 752-753
  (the CI test job), never the seat job, so "no npm step" is confirmed rather
  than inferred; a `npm ci --prefix /workspace/app` next to the pip line is
  the sibling fix.
  THIS SEAT'S RECIPE, verified, 2 commands: `python -m pip install -e plugin
  --no-deps -q` (exit 0 under the pinned HOME → lands in the pinned user site)

- Note taken on [2026-10-21 Wed 16:55] :: DEFECT FOUND + FIXED on this
  surface (seat platform-engineer, branch ai/turn-20261021-1640) — the
  runtime asset route could not address the shape every shipped brand.json
  actually uses. :DELIVERED: evidence:
  FINDING (from the first BRANCH-coverage run on plugin/src — 92% and the
  missing lines were all refusal paths, which is where the bug hid):
  app/brands/{default,freshorama,minimal-light}/brand.json all store
  "logo": "assets/logo.svg" + "favicon": "assets/icon.png", but the runtime
  route was branding/asset/<str:name> — a converter that cannot match "/".
  So reverse() raised NoReverseMatch, brandstore._asset_url's broad
  `except Exception` swallowed it and emitted the fallback URL anyway, and
  that URL 404s AT THE ROUTER: the client was handed a logo link to bytes
  that exist, with no error on either side. Second victim of the same
  flattening: brandstore._public() rewrote the stored path to
  Path(rel).name, so the branding editor round-tripped "assets/logo.svg" to
  "logo.svg" and the NEXT save answered 400 "file not found in brand dir"
  on a brand the user never touched.
  FIX (one commit, both sides of the contract — the twin law, like
  brandToCss/render_css): plugin/src/admincraft/urls.py:23 route is now
  <path:name>; plugin/src/admincraft/brandstore.py:140 _public() serves the
  WHOLE relative path (and :66 quotes the fallback tail);
  plugin/src/admincraft/views.py:90 the "/" ban becomes a shape guard
  (`.`/`..`/empty segment/absolute/backslash -> "bad asset name") with
  brandstore.asset_path()'s realpath+commonpath jail kept as the authority;
  app/src/pages/Branding.svelte:39/75 the editor keeps the stored relative
  path (ASSET_PATH_PREFIX, twin of the plugin's mount) instead of popping
  the basename.
  TESTS: +14 pytest (plugin/tests/test_branding_api.py — the shipped-shape
  PUT/GET/bytes round trip, a test that reads the REAL
  app/brands/default/brand.json + its assets, subdir 404, 7-parametrized
  shape refusals pinned to the GUARD's own message, a symlinked asset pinned
  to the JAIL's message, an embedded NUL refused instead of 500ing) and +1
  vitest (brandingMount.test.ts — untouched subdir asset keeps its dir).
  MUTANTS: m1 flatten _public, m2 revert to <str:name>, m3 except
  ValueError->KeyError, m4 client basename slice, m5 weaken the shape guard,
  m6 un-jailed asset_path — SIX seds, ZERO survivors; m5 died only because the
  message is pinned (a status-only assertion cannot tell guard from jail) and
  m6 died only on the symlink case (the guard cannot see it). Each killed its
  own named test, sources restored from /tmp/mut backups, never committed.
  GATE at this HEAD: pytest 299/299 (was 285), vitest 353/353 (was 352) over
  26 files, `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0, manage.py check "no issues
  (0 silenced)", makemigrations --check "No changes detected".
  DOCS: docs/DEVGUIDE.md new asset-relative-path law + counts,
  plugin/README.md and skills/admincraft-craft/references/branding.md now
  say <relative-path>.
  STILL OPEN: pixels (no browser/Tauri here) — a real rebrand with a subdir
  logo in a live app is unproven by inspection; board state untouched, DONE
  stays the Lead's gate, no completion claim from this seat.
